# RSC-20261104-R02 resource probe record — T1 baseline

This is the T1 baseline collected before any production `SourceLoadSession` change. It is a new record; no prior data was overwritten.

## Environment and frozen harness

- Worktree: `/data/working/qubit/rust-common/rs-config-task-r02`, branch `task/rsc-20261104-r02`.
- Starting HEAD: `d35063758551a0ca56700f7db2180cebc262e9de`; starting worktree status was clean.
- Toolchain: rustc 1.94.0 (`4a4ef493e3a1488c6e321570238084b38948f6db`), Cargo 1.94.0 (`85eff7c80`), target `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu`.
- Host: `Linux mas-server 6.8.0-147-generic #147-Ubuntu SMP PREEMPT_DYNAMIC Thu Sep 24 11:33:16 UTC 2026 x86_64 x86_64 x86_64 GNU/Linux`.
- Frozen probe SHA-256: `62c7b96d0cb85c963586616ae12010e3e27f54a6cf53ae69a68f19f71ca7926c` (`examples/config_composite_resource_probe.rs`).
- Probe test command `cargo test --locked --example config_composite_resource_probe`: exit 0, 3 passed, 0 failed.
- Release build command `cargo build --locked --release --example config_composite_resource_probe`: exit 0.
- `/usr/bin/time -v true`: exit 0; verbose time output was available. `heaptrack` was not installed (`command -v heaptrack` produced no path); no system tool was installed.
- Harness behavior: default labels preserve the preexisting probe construction; optional `--label-bytes` accepts 1 through 4096, and the 1024-byte case uses per-layer ASCII labels with decimal index suffixes. Parent argument forwarding was tested via the exact shared child-argument builder.

## Matrix coverage and outcomes

Each shape (`chain`, `wide`) covered 67 process runs: default depths 0/1/32/128/255 at 5 runs each (load success), default depths 256/257 at 1 run each (`SourceCount` rejection with normal probe teardown), raised and unbounded depths 512/1024/2048 at 5 runs each (load success), and long-label depth 255/default plus depth 1024/raised at 5 runs each (load success). Total: 134 process runs, no timeout or unexpected exit.

| Shape | Limits | Depth | Label bytes | Planned / actual runs | Load outcome |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | --- |
| chain | default | 0, 1, 32, 128, 255 | default | 5 each | all 5 successful at each depth |
| chain | default | 256, 257 | default | 1 each | `SourceCount` rejection; probe exit 0 and teardown completed |
| chain | raised | 512, 1024, 2048 | default | 5 each | all 5 successful at each depth |
| chain | unbounded | 512, 1024, 2048 | default | 5 each | all 5 successful at each depth |
| chain | default | 255 | 1024 | 5 | all 5 successful |
| chain | raised | 1024 | 1024 | 5 | all 5 successful |
| wide | default | 0, 1, 32, 128, 255 | default | 5 each | all 5 successful at each depth |
| wide | default | 256, 257 | default | 1 each | `SourceCount` rejection; probe exit 0 and teardown completed |
| wide | raised | 512, 1024, 2048 | default | 5 each | all 5 successful at each depth |
| wide | unbounded | 512, 1024, 2048 | default | 5 each | all 5 successful at each depth |
| wide | default | 255 | 1024 | 5 | all 5 successful |
| wide | raised | 1024 | 1024 | 5 | all 5 successful |

## Per-case timing and RSS summary

Elapsed time is the outer Python wall clock for each whole parent-plus-child invocation; median is across runs within each row. RSS values are `/usr/bin/time -v` maximum resident set size in KiB reported by Linux `wait4` accounting for the timed probe parent and its waited child process. A local accounting check showed that a parent waiting for one 300 MiB child reports about 300 MiB, while two concurrent 150 MiB children report about 150 MiB: this is the peak of an individual process in the waited process tree, not the simultaneous sum across processes. It is not a per-function allocation metric. The raw `/usr/bin/time` report also contains the child’s own `load_elapsed_ns`; elapsed times include process launch and teardown. RSS granularity and operating-system accounting limit interpretation; use same-host before/after comparisons rather than attributing the total to `SourceLoadSession::child` alone.

# T3 candidate — Arc shared ancestor labels

- Candidate production change: `b1ac55d60f8e25d45045630bd1bd92f37c498e35` (`SourceLoadSession::child` shares ancestor diagnostic strings with `Arc<str>`); formatter-only commits `63752d6` and `03b4926` are included in the worktree.
- Probe source SHA used to build candidate: `62c7b96d0cb85c963586616ae12010e3e27f54a6cf53ae69a68f19f71ca7926c`, exactly the frozen T1 harness SHA. It was restored from the baseline commit, verified, built with the current Arc production source, then the example source was restored to HEAD. Final example source SHA is `b16290cb80ec126aa6136bf04b48a07dded49edea43bea59bb66861ef77d16e6`; no example source diff remains.
- Environment: same worktree/host and rustc 1.94.0 / cargo 1.94.0 / x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu as T1. Candidate build command `cargo build --locked --release --example config_composite_resource_probe`: exit 0. Matrix harness was `/usr/bin/time -v <absolute candidate binary> --shape {chain|wide} --depth N --limits {default|raised|unbounded} [--label-bytes 1024]`; total 134 separate processes, completed in 5.14 seconds. All success cases loaded successfully; default N=256/257 each returned the expected `SourceCount` rejection with process exit 0. No unexpected exit or timeout. `heaptrack` remains unavailable; no tools were installed.
- Values below report outer wall-clock median and all individual elapsed/RSS values; peak RSS remains the maximum single-process peak reported by Linux `wait4`, not an aggregate across processes or a per-function allocation measure.

## T1 comparison and decision

Long-label chain RSS fell from 38,976 KiB to 4,928–5,376 KiB at N=255/default (about 86% lower; median sample 5,376 KiB), and from 566,272–566,720 KiB to 26,880–27,776 KiB at N=1024/raised (about 95% lower; median 26,880 KiB). This is a repeatable large process-RSS reduction at both long-label chain points. T1→T3 wall-time medians for shallow default-label chains N=0/1/32/128 were +65%, +10%, +67%, and +7%, while wide N=0/1/32/128 were +16%, +70%, +8%, and -8%; these first-pass values are noisy and triggered the prescribed interleaved retest.

The retest ran 5 baseline and 5 candidate processes per shallow/wide default-label case in randomized alternating order (100 processes total), with the identical frozen probe semantics and `/usr/bin/time -v`. Retest candidate/baseline median wall ratios for chain N=0/1/32/128/255 were +1.0%, +0.4%, +0.2%, -3.1%, +1.3%; for wide N=0/1/32/128/255 they were -2.7%, +0.5%, -1.0%, -3.1%, -2.3%. No repeated comparison exceeded 10%; no confirmed short-label shallow or wide regression was observed. Retest per-sample commands and outcomes follow the candidate matrix raw samples.

Conclusion: **supports retaining Arc**. The long-label chain shows a substantial, repeatable peak-RSS reduction, while interleaved shallow/wide wall-time medians show no confirmed meaningful regression. This conclusion is scoped to this harness, host, and the measured cases.

## Final T3 integration validation

The candidate production source was unchanged after the already recorded two successful runs of `./.infra/bin/align-ci.sh`; after restoring the probe source, its SHA-256 matched the current HEAD blob. Final worktree validation results:

- `./.infra/bin/style-check.sh`: exit 0; Rust style checks passed.
- `cargo check --workspace --all-targets`: exit 0.
- `./.infra/bin/ci-check.sh`: exit 0; CI reported success. This included feature-matrix test/build/doc checks, all 8 fuzz smoke targets (`config_wire` completed 134,008 runs; `properties_parser` completed 4,318 runs; the other targets also passed), examples and repository helper checks, package verification, lock/security checks, and coverage collection.
- CI coverage collection succeeded with functions 99.34% (909/915), lines 97.29% (6099/6269), and regions 95.94% (9207/9597), satisfying its configured aggregate thresholds.
- `./scripts/check-project-coverage.sh`: exit 0. Its separate `cargo llvm-cov` collection reported functions 99.13% (907/915), lines 97.13% (6089/6269), and regions 95.79% (9193/9597), satisfying aggregate thresholds; the project checker also reported `checked 93 production coverage files; used metric-specific lower bounds for 2 production coverage files`. This separate command was run because the CI coverage task did not execute the project's per-file checker.
- The repository has an independent `fuzz/` crate; its compile/run gate was exercised by `ci-check.sh` for all 8 configured targets and passed.
